Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 4 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table with per-topic hour allocations, printed pp. 6–17) plus one guide section per student chapter with objectives, teaching guidance and answer keys (printed pp. 1–252). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 167:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SMP/MTs Kelas IX* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Yosep Dwi Kristanto, Muhammad Taqiyuddin, Elyda Yulfiana, Indra Rukmana, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-901-1. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 9 student book cited in this lesson — the Fase D learning-outcome flow for Grade 9, lesson schemes, teaching guidance per sub-section, technology integration (Desmos, GeoGebra, CODAP) and worked answer keys for every Latihan, Uji Kompetensi and Proyek of the student book (printed pp. 1–276). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its contents misprints the Bab III and Bab IV starts as pp. 147 and 207; the chapters open at printed pp. 146 and 206. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| Worked problem: truncated cone whose axial-section midline is 12 cm and generator 5 cm → lateral area 60π cm² (trapezium midline theorem used); control questions ask for the total-area formula (not stated in the text) | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 79 |
| Concept of volume: unit cube as unit of volume; cm³, m³, km³; volume defined as the number of unit volumes the solid contains (non-integer allowed) | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 93 |
| Two volume postulates accepted a priori: every solid has a unique positive volume; additivity — volume of a whole = sum of volumes of non-overlapping parts; solids of equal volume ("равновеликие") vs congruent solids distinguished | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 93 |
| Theorem 21 (proved from an unproved lemma that cuboids on equal bases have volumes as their heights): volume of a cuboid V = abc; worked problem: increasing a cube's edge by 1 m increases its volume by 7 m³ → edge 1 m (quadratic equation) | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 95 |
| Corollaries: volume of a cuboid = base area × height; volume of a cube V = a³; statement (proof omitted) that V = S·H holds for every parallelepiped, oblique included | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 96 |
| Worked problem: volumes from three face areas — if the face areas are Q1, Q2, Q3 then V = √(Q1Q2Q3); areas 2, 3, 6 m² → V = 6 m³ | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 96 |
| Theorem 22 (proved): volume of a right prism = base area × height — first for a right-triangle base by completing to a cuboid, then any triangular base by splitting, then any prism by triangulation; extension to oblique prisms stated in a remark (used in the next worked problems) | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 98 |
| Worked problems: right prism split by a plane through a lateral edge perpendicular to a face, using Euclid's altitude relations in the right-triangle base; oblique prism on a rhombus base (diagonals 5 and 6, height 10) → 150 cm³ — taught corrected: the sub-prism's volume line names the wrong base triangle, S_ABC for S_ABD (the values 200, 40, 160 cm³ are right); and exercise 248 (printed p. 102) — a cast-iron pipe of outer width 2.5 cm with 3 cm walls cannot exist; the key's 192.72 kg is exactly the value for an outer width of 25 cm. | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 100 |
| Theorem 23 (proved): volume of a pyramid = one third of base area × height — triangular case by completing to a prism split into three pyramids of equal volume (equal volumes asserted from equal bases and heights), general case by triangulating the base | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 103 |
| Worked problems: square pyramid, height 9, base edge 8 → 192 cm³; volumes in terms of the edge a: regular tetrahedron √2a³/12, regular square pyramid √2a³/6; regular octahedron as two such pyramids → √2a³/3, and for a = 9 cm, 243√2 cm³ | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 104 |
| Theorem 24 (proved by completing the frustum to a pyramid and using similarity of areas): volume of a truncated pyramid = H/3 · (S1 + √(S1S2) + S2) — one third of the height times the sum of the base areas and their geometric mean | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 107 |
| Worked problem: truncated pyramid with square bases of sides 8 and 5 cm and height 6 cm → V = 258 cm³ | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 108 |

| Applied worked problem: three cylindrical cans at the same price — existing can, a can of half the radius and double the height, a can of double the radius and half the height → volumes 8πa²b, 4πa²b, 16πa²b; the widest can is the best buy | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 113 |
| Worked problem: two similar cylinders with total volume 140 cm³ and lateral areas in ratio 4:9 → volumes 32 cm³ and 108 cm³ (area ratio k², volume ratio k³) | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 113 |
| Theorem 26 (proved by the integral formula with the generator line y = (R/H)x): volume of a right cone = one third of base area × height, V = πR²H/3; remark: also true for an oblique cone (proof omitted); the text misstates the bounding line as x = R for x = H — taught corrected: the proof's prose (printed p. 116) bounds the generating triangle by the line x = R; it is x = H (the integral and the result are right). | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 115 |
| Worked problems: cone with generator 6 cm at 60° to the base → V = 9√3π cm³; cone whose axial section is a right-angled isosceles triangle of area 9 m² → R = H = 3 m, V = 9π m³ — the answer key to exercise 295 (printed p. 141) is taught corrected: it prints 2π cm³; the sand-pile problem (printed p. 118) is in metres — 2π m³ (the value is right, the unit wrong). | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 116 |
| Theorem 27 (proved twice: by the integral formula and by completing the cone with similarity): volume of a truncated cone = H/3 · (S1 + √(S1S2) + S2) = πH/3 · (R1² + R1R2 + R2²) | Tajikistan, Геометрия (Grade 11), printed p. 119 |

| Volume measured by counting unit cubes; volume of a cuboid = length × width × height; volume of a cube = edge × edge × edge (worked: edge 3 gives 27); the cubic centimetre used for small volumes and the litre for larger ones; converting between volume units (the cubic metre and its parts and multiples); naming solids from models and pictures — cuboid, cube, cylinder, pyramid, cone, prism — and counting a polyhedron by its faces, vertices and edges (a cuboid has 6 faces, 12 edges, 8 vertices; a triangular prism 5, 9, 6); the cylinder's and cone's curved surfaces described; building a solid from its net and drawing a net for a given solid | Syria, Grade 5 pupil's book, printed pp. 113–118, 162–166 |
| Volume of a cube = edge × edge × edge (worked: edge 3 gives 27) and volume of a cuboid = length × width × height (worked: 6 × 3 × 3 = 54); the right prism and the cylinder met through their unfoldings — a right prism's net built from its bases and rectangular faces, a cylinder's net from two circles and a rectangle (the right prism's net and the cylinder's net are drawn in the unit's lessons) | Syria, Grade 6 pupil's book, printed pp. 154, 168–172 |
| Lateral surface area of a right prism = perimeter of the base × the height, seen by unwrapping the lateral faces into one rectangle (the gift-box wrapping activity); total surface area = lateral area + twice the base area; volume of a right prism = area of the base × the height, volume of a cuboid the product of its three dimensions, volume of a cube (edge)³ — a right prism on a right-triangle base with legs 3 and 4 and height 5 has volume 6 × 5 = 30; a room as a prism problem (walls' area = base perimeter 28 m × height 3.5 m = 98 m² of wallpaper; with the ceiling, 142 m²); the net of a cylinder of revolution — the label paper is a rectangle whose length is the circumference of the base; lateral area of a cylinder = 2πrh, total area adds twice the base area, volume = πr²h (worked in full with π = 3.14: height 40, base diameter 15 — lateral area 1,884, total area 2,237.25, volume 7,065); three cylinders with radii 8, 7, 6 and heights 10.5, 12, 14 have equal lateral areas (2π × 84) but different volumes | Syria, Grade 7 student's book, printed pp. 154–162 |
| The volume of a pyramid is V = ⅓ × (base area) × height, motivated by dissecting a cube of edge a into six congruent regular pyramids on its faces, each of volume a³/6; the formula used in both directions (height 15 with a square base of side 12 gives V = 720 cm³; from V = 156 and height 36 the base area is 13); the volume of a cone of revolution is V = ⅓ × (base area) × height = ⅓πr²h, given as accepted (نقبل); cone volume worked with Pythagoras first (a candle cone of base diameter 10 and generator 13 has height 12 by the 5-12-13 triangle, and volume 100π ≈ 314 cm³); finding a cone's height from its volume (V = 50 with r = 3 gives h ≈ 5.3 cm) | Syria, Grade 8 Geometry student's book, printed pp. 102–108 |
| Volume of a prism or a cylinder equals base area × height; cuboid V = x × y × z; cube V = x³ (worked cylinder volume: V = π × 7.5² × 40 = 2250π cm³); volume of a pyramid V = (1/3) × base area × height, and of a right circular cone V = (1/3) × base area × height (worked: r = 1.5 cm, h = 4 cm gives 3π cm³); the sphere defined as the set of points M with OM = R and the solid ball as OM ≤ R, with diameter, great circle and antipodal points; Archimedes' results used to obtain the sphere formulas — the sphere's surface area equals the lateral area of its circumscribing cylinder, and the sphere's volume equals two-thirds of that cylinder's volume — giving S = 4πR² (worked: diameter 60 cm gives S = 3600π cm²) and V = (4/3)πR³; the Earth treated as a sphere — from Eratosthenes' arc measurement (7.2° of the meridian for the Syene–Alexandria distance) the Earth's radius comes out R ≈ 6,366 km, and a quarter of the equator measures 10,000 km, the historical origin of the metre; a section of a solid by a plane defined, with sections of cuboid, cylinder, cone, pyramid and sphere by planes parallel to a face, an edge, the axis or the base — a cone or pyramid section parallel to the base is a reduction of the base (ratios by Thales; a cone section with k = 0.6 has area 0.6² × 16π = 5.76π cm²), and the part between the section and the base is the frustum (جذع الهرم), whose volume V = (1/3) h (S + S′ + √(S × S′)) is checked on a worked example (83.25 cm³) | Syria, Grade 9 Geometry student's book, printed pp. 62–75, 77–82 |
| Volume by cross-sections (activity): for a solid between the planes x = a and x = b, V = ∫ₐᵇ S(x) dx with S(x) the cross-section area (formula accepted) — the sphere's volume re-derived from S(x) = π(R² − x²), and a solid of revolution about the x-axis has disc sections S(x) = π f(x)²; the volume of a paraboloid of revolution bracketed by stacks of cylinders (an enrichment activity in the sequence-limits unit) | Syria, Grade 12 Scientific, Part One, printed pp. 122, 223 |

| The cube's net — based on the previous practical work, write answers to the questions: a. how many sticks were needed to make a cube? What does that say about the cube?; b. what did the solid made from plasticine joined with sticks turn out to be?; c. can a cube-shaped solid be made from paper? How are a square and a cube different?; look at a cube-shaped box's net (дэлгээс): prepare a cube-shaped box and scissors: work carefully: a. unfold the box and observe it: the box's "net" was formed (pictures show a box being opened out into a cross-shaped net); b. is there a pupil who unfolded the box in a different way from the picture? How many differently-shaped nets can be formed? How do you know? From how many of what shapes does the net consist?; c. which faces of the net are equal to each other? Discuss and draw a conclusion; make a cube-shaped box from squares of side 4 cm: how many squares must be prepared? a. join the squares with sticky tape and make the cube's net; b. check whether the net you made is right: join the faces with tape; c. can the cube's net be made in another way? Try making it; build a cube of edge 2 cm from its net: prepare paper, glue, scissors, a pencil and a ruler: compare carefully with the picture: a. draw the net as shown in the picture (a large net drawn on squared paper, its squares labelled with face names); b. draw the net leaving the parts for gluing; c. cut out the drawn pattern, fold it along the drawn lines and build the cube; d. without counting the gluing parts, find the net's area measure by looking at the picture; e. discuss the method of finding the net's area measure you found | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 78 |
| Nets: area of a cube's net; counting cubes — a test (Сорих): write the name of the pictured solid and complete its properties (a cube is pictured): the solid's name: …; the solid has □ faces, □ vertices and □ edges; all the edges' lengths are …; from which of the pictured shapes can a cube's net be made (two arrangements of 1 cm squares are shown)? Find the cube net's area measure; consolidation (Бататгах): discuss the difference between a shape and a solid: a. in the picture, which is a shape and which is a solid (a flat yellow square and a yellow cube)? How are they alike and how are they different?; b. hold the solid and the shape in your hand and check; c. find cube-shaped things around you; which of the nets can be used to build a cube (four cross-like nets a-d)? Explain how you knew; what other shapes can a cube's net have?; in the following cases, find the cube net's area measure: a. one face's area measure is 36 cm.kv (6 × 36 = 216 cm.kv); b. the edge length is 5 cm; c. the edge length is 7 cm; d. one face's perimeter is 12 cm; find the area measure: make a cube with the given measurement and imagine it is a "house": make and glue a door and windows: find the walls' and the floor's area measure (the cube's edge is 8 cm; a window is a 2 cm square; a door is 2 cm by 3 cm); write the number of cubes (five solids built from stacked cubes are pictured — count the cubes in each, including the hidden ones) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 79 |
| Nets of the cube and the cylinder — making a solid's net (Биетийн дэлгээс хийх): observe the pictures and match them with the similar solids: discuss how many faces, vertices and edges each solid has (the photographs: a gift box, a tissue box, a book standing like a tent and a candle; the wireframe drawings are named: cube (Куб), cylinder (Цилиндр), parallelepiped (Параллелепипед) and triangular-based prism (Гурвалжин суурьтай призм)); an activity: 1. let's make a cube's net: a. take apart a cube-shaped box; b. draw the unfolded picture (the net) on prepared paper and cut round it (a cube and its opened flower-like net are pictured); c. the net consists of how many squares (6)?; 2. from the following pictures, find which of them can be a cube's net (five arrangements of squares: a cross, a column with side tabs, a zigzag strip, a staircase, and a 2 × 4 block); 3. let's make a cylinder's net: a. take apart a cylinder-shaped box and observe the unfolded picture; b. what shapes does the net consist of (a rectangle and 2 circles, as pictured)? | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 23 |
| Surface area from nets: cube and parallelepiped — let's learn to find the area of the nets of the cube and the parallelepiped (pictures of an open box, a matchbox, a gift box and a stack of coloured books): work following the instructions: a. draw the parallelepiped's net in your notebook; b. mark each face in the table and find the total area: which faces have equal areas (the net is drawn on a grid with its faces lettered, one cell counting as 1 cm.kv; the table lists the faces with their areas, face A given as 2 cm.kv, and a total row); c. measure the faces' edges and mark the net's edges: which edges are equal in length?; draw the cube's net in your notebook and find its surface area measure (a boy's note: "The sum of the area measures of all the parallelepiped's faces is called its surface area measure"); an activity: draw a parallelepiped's net in your notebook and find its surface area measure: a. take a parallelepiped-shaped paper box and unfold it along its edges as shown in the picture; b. on the paper, draw round the net along its edges; c. measure its edges using a ruler and mark them; d. find the faces' area measures and find their sum (the pictured net has its six faces numbered and coloured); a self-check: a parallelepiped's edges are 2 cm, 6 cm and 8 cm: find its surface area measure (152 cm.kv); find the parallelepipeds' surface area measures (a. a gridded block 4 by 3 with height 5; b. a cube of edge 3; c. a tall parallelepiped of base 3 and height 11; d. a parallelepiped 7 long, 2 wide and 4 high); a parallelepiped's surface area measure is 192 cm.kv: find the unknown edge's length and discuss (the pictured parallelepiped has one edge 4 and the height unknown); three parallelepipeds' surface area measures are equal: find the lengths of the unknown edges a and c (a column 4 cm by 4 cm and 8 cm tall; a flat box 6 cm by 6 cm with unknown height a; a cube of edge 2 cm); if one edge of the parallelepiped given in the picture (12 cm by 9 cm by 7 cm) is decreased 3 times, how will its surface area measure change? Discuss; find the nets' area measures (a. a cube net with edge 6; b. a parallelepiped net with panels measured 5, 2 and 8); a puzzle: to complete the pictured composition into a cube, how many more small cubes must be added (a stepped pyramid of purple cubes)? Find the surface area measure of the cube formed | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 24–25 |
| Measuring a solid's volume (Биетийн эзлэхүүнийг хэмжих): a. build a rectangular parallelepiped and a cube out of unit cubes and name them (the table: with a cubes' count (Кубийн тоо) of 4, the pictured builds — 4 in a row and the flat 2 by 2 — and the cube's and parallelepiped's measures (хэмжээс) to name; with 8 cubes, the builds including the 2 × 2 × 2 cube, with the measures given: a. 2 × 2 × 2; b. 4 × 1 × 2; c. 8 × 1 × 1; the explanation: a parallelepiped's length, width and height are called its measures: for example, written 4 × 2 × 1 means this parallelepiped's length is 4, its width 2 and its height 1 unit; b. with 6 unit cubes, how many different parallelepipeds can be built? Can a cube be built? Write their measures); the definition box: "Every solid has a volume (Эзлэхүүн). Roughly, a volume is measured by a cube whose edge is 1 unit (1 cm, 1 dm, 1 m, 1 km) long, and it is called a unit cube. If a cube's edge is 1 cm long it is said to have a volume of 1 cm.kub (cm³); if its edge is 2 cm long, a volume of 8 cm.kub"; if the solid is completed into a parallelepiped, how many unit cubes will it form? What is its volume (the pictured open tray: the base is 6 × 3 = 18 cubes; since 3 layers are laid on top, 3 × 18 = 54 cubes: so its volume is 6 × 3 × 3, that is, 54 unit cubes)?; calculate mentally (a. with 9 cubes of 1 cm edge, how many different parallelepipeds can be built: are their measures the same? Tell them; b. how many dice of 1 cm edge fit inside a cube of 3 cm edge (27)?); solids were built by stacking cubes of 1 cm.kub measure (a. which solid has the biggest volume?; b. how many cubes must be added to make the pictured solid Б into a parallelepiped of measure 3 × 2 × 3 (the four pictured cube structures A, Б, B and Г)?); find the number of the picture of the solid as seen from above (the cube structure with its four top-view grids to choose from); find the volumes of the following solids (a. the green cube structure built of 1 cm cubes; b. the wireframe cube with its layer of green unit cubes; c. the blue solid of 5 cm by 3 cm by 4 cm); find the units for measuring volume and write them (a. cm; b. cm.kv; c. cm.kub; d. m.kv; e. m.kub — choosing which units measure length, area and volume); calculate how many dice of 1 cm.kub fit into the following rectangular parallelepipeds (a. the length 8 cm, the width 4 cm, the height 2 cm (64); b. the width 5 cm, the length 7 cm, the height 10 cm (350)); a parallelepiped's volume is 900 cm.kub: its length is 15 cm and its width 10 cm: find the measure of its height (6 cm); a self-check: a classroom of rectangular-parallelepiped shape has the measure 10 × 7 × 3 (m): in this class 42 children study: how many cubic metres of air fall to one child (210 ÷ 42 = 5 m³)? | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 139–140 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 65 countries (Kazakhstan, Jordan, South Africa, Egypt, Greece, Armenia, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, Libya, Vietnam, Ghana, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, North Macedonia, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Turkey, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Belarus, Australia, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Mozambique, Nigeria).**.** Sierra Leone carries the cleanest Iran gives the cuboid's volume in Grade 5, prism volumes and the cylinder's lateral area in Grade 7, and the sphere, pyramid and cone in Grade 9; its Grade 10 Geometry (1) treats the solids themselves without computing their volumes.
ladder — cubes, prisms and cylinders by cross-section × height,
then surface areas by the net, in JSS 2. Egypt carries volume by
counting unit cubes through V = l × w × h in Grade 5 and the
cuboid, prism and pyramid surface areas in Grade 6, surface area
and volume of a solid in Prep 2's new Term 2 book, and surface
area and volume of a sphere in Grade 11 Applied (rows completed
by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep). Jordan
carries the prism and pyramid in Grade 5, the rectangular prism's
volume in Grade 6, the prism/cylinder and pyramid/cone volumes
and surface areas in Grade 7, and the sphere in Grade 8.
Vietnam carries volume units, nets, surface areas and volumes of
the box and the cube in Grade 5. Libya carries volume from
litres in Grade 2 through cuboids in Grade 4 to the 8×4×5 cm
cuboid in Grade 8. Kazakhstan carries the parallelepiped's edge
length and volume in Grade 4 and the cuboid/cube volume formulas
in Grade 5. Mauritania carries cube area and volume and the
cylinder in Grade 6 and the cube, pavé and prism in 1AS. South
Africa carries surface area, volume and capacity of cubes,
prisms and cylinders in Grades 8 and 9. Greece carries volume by
counting unit cubes in Grade 4 and prism/cylinder surface area
and volume in Grade 8. Armenia carries volume in Grade 6.
Ghana's evidence is teacher-manual only (JICA B5 Lessons 92–100
and B6 Lessons 88–92, prisms): no pupil textbook in Ghana's set
teaches volume or surface area, and the lesson words Ghana that
way throughout. Mauritania's honest limit is stated too: its
Grade 5 book teaches no volume at all. Laos carries volume and surface area from solids, nets and volume formulas in Grade 5, through prisms, cylinders and spheres in Grade 8, to pyramid and cone surface area and volume in Grade 9 and volumes of revolution in Grade 10. Yemen carries volume and surface area from volumes in Grade 3, through surface area and volume in Grade 6, to volumes of pyramids, cones and spheres in Grades 7–8. Indonesia carries volume and surface area from volume by counting unit cubes in Grade 4, to surface area and volume of all seven solids — cube, cuboid, prism, pyramid, cylinder, cone, sphere — with the effect of scale change in Grade 9. Honest negative: Grade 6 teaches no volume and no surface area of cubes/cuboids — its solids chapter is purely spatial. Nicaragua carries volume and surface area from prism and cube volume in Grade 6, through surface area and volume of all five solid families — prisms, pyramids, cylinders, cones, spheres — in Grade 8, to cone and pyramid volumes by Pythagoras in Grade 9 and the regular polyhedra and solids of revolution in Grade 10. Honest negative: Grade 5 classifies prisms and cylinders and draws their nets but computes neither surface area nor volume there. Ethiopia carries volume and surface area from volume by counting unit cubes and the rectangular-prism volume in Grade 5, through surface area and volume of prisms, pyramids and cylinders in Grade 8, to the full solid-figures chapter — prisms, pyramids, cones, spheres and frustums, with Cavalieri's principle proved by dissection — in Grade 10. No volume or surface-area formulae in Grade 6, and none in Grade 7. South Sudan carries volume and surface area from volume by counting cubes in Grade 4 (no cm³/m³ there), through cube and cuboid volume in Grade 5, surface area in Grade 7, cube/cuboid surface area and cylinder volume in Grade 8, to surface area and volume formulae for prisms, pyramids, cones and spheres in Secondary 1 — with the Primary 4 volume example's contradictory "40 × 11 = 440" corrected to 40 × 6 = 240, the Primary 8 cuboid surface-area formula corrected to 2(LW + LH + WH), and the Primary 8 rule "divide by 10,000" for cm³→m³ corrected to divide by 1,000,000. Iraq carries volume and surface area from measuring volume by counting unit cubes in Grade 3, through cube and cuboid volumes, lateral and total surface area, scale effects and composite solids in Grade 7, to cylinder and sphere in Grade 8 and pyramid and cone in Grade 9, with volumes of revolution named in Grade 12 Scientific at opener level only — and the two Grade 7 end-exercise errors taught corrected (486 cm² and 56 cm², neither among the printed options). Bangladesh carries volume and surface area from volume in litres and cubic units in Grade 5, through measuring the planes of a box and making 3-D models in Grade 6, cuboid surface area and volume and the volume of a right circular cylinder in Grade 7, to cone, cylinder, sphere, prism and pyramid surface areas and volumes in Grade 9 — the cylinder with its surface area and volume and the cone-vs-cylinder filling experiment (printed pp. 190–193); corrected by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep: an earlier version of this text wrongly said Grade 9 states no cylinder formulas. The honest limit that stands: Grade 9 states no cuboid/cube formulas, so the cuboid formulas in this lesson rest on Grade 7, and the Grade 9 Chapter 8 errors are taught corrected (the cone derivation assumes a 90° sector, the hemisphere's total surface area 3πr² is never stated in the book, and the pyramid lateral-area formula holds only for regular pyramids). Nepal carries volume and surface area from volume by counting unit cubes in Grades 3–4, through volume of cubes and cuboids in Grade 5, cuboid and cube volume in Grade 6, cuboid and cube total surface area in Grade 7, cube and cuboid volume in Grade 8, to prism, cylinder and sphere surface area and volume in Grade 9 and cylinder, sphere, prism and pyramid surface area and volume in Grade 10. Cambodia carries volume and surface area whole from volume units and cuboids, cylinders and cones in Grade 6 through prisms, pyramids and spheres in Grades 8–9, with the volume and lateral-area table in Grade 10. Kuwait carries volume from exploring volume in Grade 4, through volume in the Grade 6 measurement unit, to volume and surface area of prisms in Grade 7 and of prisms, cylinders and cones in Grade 8. Band C adds cone surface area and pyramid and sphere volumes in Grade 9 Term 2. (Grade 11's solid-geometry unit is synthetic theorems only, with no surface-area or volume content, so it is not stocked here.) North Macedonia carries volume and surface area whole in Grade 8 (2010 edition) — prism, pyramid, cylinder, cone and sphere, areas and volumes alike.

China carries volume and surface area whole — cuboid and cube work in Grade 5, cylinders and cones in Grade 6, and the full senior solid-geometry formulas in both editions.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries volume and surface area in Classes 6–8's cube, cuboid, cylinder, prism, pyramid, sphere, hemisphere and cone formulas, and the Class 12 triple-product volumes of the parallelepiped and tetrahedron.

Syria counts unit cubes for volume in Grade 5, unwraps prisms and cylinders for their surface areas in Grade 7, motivates the pyramid's third by dissecting a cube in Grade 8, and gathers prism, cylinder, pyramid, cone and sphere — with Archimedes' cylinder results and plane sections — in Grade 9 Geometry; Grade 12 re-derives the sphere's volume by cross-sections.

Saudi Arabia introduces volume by filling solids with unit cubes in Grade 3 — estimate, then count — and in Grade 4 counts cm³ including the hidden cubes, witnessed by the older 1445 Term-3 volumes, with practice evidence from the partial exercises book.

Malaysia builds surface area from nets in Form 2 before giving any formula, takes volumes in the same chapter, and closes the topic in Additional Mathematics Form 5 with volumes of revolution about both axes.

Rwanda teaches volume and surface area from Year 4's unit-cube cuboid (with its Teacher's Guide working the matchbox pile), through Year 5's missing dimensions and Year 6's cuboid surface area and cylinder volume, to Senior 1's full run of surface areas and volumes for prisms, pyramids, cones, cylinders and spheres.

Sudan splits the topic by design: Grade 8 teaches surface areas only — cuboid, cube, cylinder, cone, pyramid and sphere, with no volumes — and Grade 9 teaches the volumes, with units and litre conversions, the sphere's volume derived by a pyramid-dissection argument.

India stocks volume and surface area from Math-Magic Class 5 and Mathematics Class 8–10: displacement; surface area of cuboid = 2(lb + bh + hl); cuboid and cube surface area; combined solids.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик III, IV, V, VI, VII, VIII, IX, X (Grades 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10): the cube's net; nets: area of a cube's net; counting cubes; nets of the cube and the cylinder.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6), *Conciencia Matemática* (Grade 8), *La Matemática de la Belleza* (Grade 9) and *La Matemática y el Vivir Bien* (Grade 11). From its books: from Grade 6: unit cubes — Volume as space occupied, measured in unit cubes; mm³, cm³, dm³, m³ as cubes of that edge; from Grade 9: volume by experiment — a cone holds exactly one third of the same-base, same-height cylinder (sand filling); the book also derives the sphere as 2/3 of the circumscribing cylinder; from Grade 11: a solid of revolution defined: a plane region rotated about an axis; cylinder, cone and sphere generated by rotating a rectangle, a right triangle and a semicircle (hands-on card constructions); axis, height, radius, generatrix.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 6: units of volume and their conversions; the cube: total surface area and volume; the cuboid and its volume; from Grade 8: area and volume of a cuboid; diagonal of a cuboid and of a cube; area and volume of a prism; area and volume of a cylinder; area and volume of a pyramid; area and volume of a cone; surface area and volume of a sphere; chapter key points and exercises (areas and volumes).

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Liquid volume/capacity (litre, millilitre); exercises with liquid volume. from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .3 Volume of cube and cuboid (plan 12); .5 Surface area of cube and cuboid (plan 14). from Grade 9 (teacher's book): Volume and surface area of a right prism and of a cylinder; cross-section of a prism. from Grade 10 (teacher's book): Volume and surface area of prisms; Cylinders and spheres: volume and surface area KEY error in the ditare's printed answer: the sphere volume is…; Pyramids and cones: volume and surface area.

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 8: Áreas y volúmenes; Cono y esfera; Ejemplos de áreas y volúmenes; Pirámide, cilindro y esfera trabajados; Cono aplicado y ejercicios; from the Compendium's Secundaria 2 section (Grade 8): Las formas en el espacio tridimensional.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Volume units; from Grade 11: Right prism and oblique prism defined; Theorem 4 (proved): lateral surface area of a right prism = perimeter of base × height; Total surface area of a prism S = S_lat + 2S_base; Worked problems: total area of a regular hexagonal prism (base 6, height 8); Total surface area of a right parallelepiped; Total surface area of a cuboid S = 2(ab + ac + bc); Theorem 10 (proved): lateral surface area of a regular pyramid = half the base perimeter…; Regular truncated pyramid: bases are regular polygons, lateral faces are congruent…; Worked problems on cylinder sections: diagonal of the axial section for R = 2 m, H = 3 m…; Net (development) of a cylinder's lateral surface is a rectangle; Total surface area of a cylinder S = 2πR² + 2πRH = 2πR(R + H); Worked problems: cylinder R = 6 cm, H = 4 cm → S_lat = 48π cm², total printed as 120 cm²; Applied exercise set: painting a cylindrical barrel (diameter 1.5 m, height 3 m, 200 g of…; Duplicate exercise pair: area of the mid-height parallel section of a truncated cone with…; Net of a cone's lateral surface is a circular sector of radius = generator and arc = base…; Total surface area of a cone S = πRl + πR² = πR(l + R); Worked problems: cone R = 6 cm, H = 8 cm → generator 10, S_lat = 60π cm²; Applied exercise set: conical tent (height 3.5 m, base diameter 4 m); Theorem 17 (proved by completing the cone): lateral area of a truncated cone = π(R + r)l,…; Worked problem: truncated cone whose axial-section midline is 12 cm and generator 5 cm →…; Concept of volume: unit cube as unit of volume; Two volume postulates accepted a priori: every solid has a unique positive volume; Theorem 21 (proved from an unproved lemma that cuboids on equal bases have volumes as…; Corollaries: volume of a cuboid = base area × height; Worked problem: volumes from three face areas; Theorem 22 (proved): volume of a right prism = base area × height; Worked problems: right prism split by a plane through a lateral edge perpendicular to a…; Theorem 23 (proved): volume of a pyramid = one third of base area × height; Worked problems: square pyramid, height 9, base edge 8 → 192 cm³; Theorem 24 (proved by completing the frustum to a pyramid and using similarity of areas):…; Worked problem: truncated pyramid with square bases of sides 8 and 5 cm and height 6 cm →…; Theorem 25: volume of a right cylinder V = πR²H = S_base · H, derived from formula (1)…; Applied worked problem: three cylindrical cans at the same price; Worked problem: two similar cylinders with total volume 140 cm³ and lateral areas in…; Theorem 26 (proved by the integral formula with the generator line y = (R/H)x): volume of…; Worked problems: cone with generator 6 cm at 60° to the base → V = 9√3π cm³; Theorem 27 (proved twice: by the integral formula and by completing the cone with…; Applied worked problem: a cylindrical barrel (height 1.9 m, base diameter 1 m) holds…; Theorem 28: volume of a ball V = 4πR³/3, derived by the integral formula from the…; Applied worked problem: an ice-cream ball (scoop) of diameter 6 cm melting into a cone of…; Surface area defined rigorously-by-limit: area of a surface F is the limit of V_h/(2h)…; Surface area of a sphere S = 4πR², derived from the shell between concentric spheres of…

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 3 syllabus requires surface area — in the targets and body, the surface area of a cube or cuboid; Class 4 maintains surface area in the scope and sequence. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 7: Net of a cube drawn on squared paper and matched to the edges that must be cut; Nets of rectangular prisms and square prisms from real boxes (perfume, chocolate, medicine boxes); opposite faces identified and marked; Testing whether a drawn net folds up: opposite faces can never be side by side in a valid net; Surface area of a rectangular prism as the sum of its face areas: A = 2(ab + ac + bc), derived from a labelled net; Square prism and cube as special rectangular prisms; surface areas 2a² + 4ab and 6a²; Volume as capacity compared by filling boxes completely with equal objects; non-equal objects cannot compare capacities; Volume counted in unit cubes; a prism's volume is the number of unit cubes that fill it with no gaps; Volume formula built layer by layer: base area (product of the two base edges) times height, V = (a·b)·c in any orientation; Same 18 cubes rearranged into different rectangular prisms keep the same volume; Volume of a square prism and of a cube with the same base-area × height relation; Volume of an irregular solid by water displacement in a graduated cylinder.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Geometry 11; Geometry 10: idea of a figure of revolution; the right circular cylinder; surface area of the cylinder; the concept of the cone; surface area of the cone; the frustum of a cone; about the prism and the pyramid (reference treatment); parallelepiped; rectangular parallelepiped; pyramid; regular pyramid.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 19 teacher-evidence items from 8 of the guides (Book 6, Book 7, Book 19, Book 20, Book 22, Book 23, Book 24, Book 25; Grades 5, 6, 7, 8, 9).

**Book 6:** *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 1ère année du cycle secondaire collégial* (1AC = Grade 7, BIOF) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 3,742,780 bytes, 173 PDF pages. Chapters 1–2 are method framework (excluded in the placements); items are the distinct topics of the 1AC programme distribution (printed p. 73). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 8 teacher-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. **Book 5:** *Matemática — Manual da 5.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 5) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-84-8; 4,730,235 bytes, 128 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 29's worked example 12 + 37 + 7 + 3 is printed as = 55 with a garbled regrouping (correct total: 59); printed p. 73's equivalence line prints 1/3 = 2/6 = 2/9 (the last term should be 3/9); printed p. 127's median worked example uses an ordered list containing 12, which is not in the exercise's data, and drops one 10 (the median is taught from the data as printed in the exercise: ordered 6, 7, 9, 10, 10, 10, 11, 13, 14 → median 10).

**Book 6:** *Matemática — Manual da 6.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 6) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-85-5; 2,544,284 bytes, 104 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book notes, taught corrected: printed p. 51 first presents the ambiguous expression 28 : 7 × 2 with two candidate readings and tells the pupil to ask the teacher — the rule is fixed by the worked examples on printed p. 52, and the course teaches the standard order (× and ÷ in order, before + and −); printed p. 62's direct-proportionality worked line prints the third ratio with denominator 6 where the sequence's term is 5 (150/5 = 30 — taught from the sequence). The book carries printer's proof marks, and one running footer misprints “Matemática 5ª Classe” (printed p. 99) — production matter, cited nowhere.

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 11 requirement-evidence items from Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). SYLLABUS EVIDENCE ONLY — an examination syllabus states what candidates are required to know; it teaches nothing itself.

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Belize's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 documents, all of them Belize Ministry of Education *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide* books (August 2012) — Infant 1, Standard 1 and Standard IV; 0 pupil (student) textbooks** — and placed 108 teaching items. Every document in the set is a teacher's guide: it states the learning outcomes pupils must reach and teaches the teacher how to teach each one (comments, sample lesson objectives, specific content, sample activities and assessment). Every Belize item in this section is therefore TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE — Belize's guides teach teachers how to teach the topic; no Belize pupil textbook is in hand — cited as the evidence kind it is; Belize is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until a Belize pupil book is in hand and read. No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 3 teacher-guide-evidence items from Book 1 and Book 3.

**Book 1:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Infant 1* (teacher guide, Grade 1) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 738,164 bytes, 58 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–52. Printed page = PDF page. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

**Book 3:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard IV* (teacher guide, Grade 6) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 868,323 bytes, 51 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–15; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 16–45. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series (M1.16 onwards). The detailed sections of this guide are markedly thinner than in Books 1–2 (several state “Specific Content: N/A”); placements rest on the outcomes as stated. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

## Australia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Australia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 books, all of them student textbooks** (Years 8–10: eight Cambridge volumes — the Essential Mathematics Australian Curriculum, Victorian Curriculum and CambridgeMATHS NSW lines, plus the GOLD support edition and the CORE pathway volume — and one Jacaranda volume, Maths Quest 10+10A) — and placed 844 teaching items. Australia has no government textbook series (ACARA writes the Australian Curriculum but publishes and prescribes no textbooks); the set is the school-used commercial series the country records name, and every book is cited as such. All 9 files are distinct works (distinct ISBNs, extents and contents, verified per book on the read); the publisher-line relations are stated in the placements: Book A1 (GOLD) is the support edition of Book A2's Year 8 material, Book A3 is the same line in its Victorian ©2024 edition, Books A4 and A6 are the NSW syllabus editions, and Book A9 is Book A8's CORE pathway companion. In the sections below the books are cited as Books A1–A9. Australia COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every item is pupil-textbook teaching. The read's four flagged claims are carried exactly: (F1) Book A3's contents lists its Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the file in hand contains the full chapter, bound after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941) — it is read and placed, and it founds this course's algorithmic-thinking lesson; (F2) Book A7's contents lists a Chapter 18, Algorithmic thinking, with no page numbers, and the chapter is NOT in the file (searched end to end; the book closes with its Glossary, printed p. 1106, and Index, printed p. 1117) — recorded as absent, not read, and no item rests on it; (F3) Book A9's running head at printed p. 644 prints section 10E's code as “0E” — the book's own production error; the placement cites 10E, as the section's heading and contents give it; (F4) only the 9 Maths books are this read's set — the country records' wider Australia picture (12 staged books across subjects, plus filled-by-exact-link titles) is not merged into this count, and in particular the two senior Maths titles that exist only as exact-link records (Cambridge Senior Specialist Mathematics, QCE Units 3&4, 2e, and Cambridge Senior Mathematical Methods, QCE Units 3&4, 2e) are NOT in hand and were NOT read. The placements' named exclusions — front matter and “how to use this book” guides; every chapter's pre-tests, progress quizzes, unlettered applications / problem-solving / modelling / investigation spreads, puzzles and games, chapter summaries, checklists and reviews, semester reviews, glossaries, indexes and answer sections — are apparatus/assessment, teach no new content, and are stocked nowhere. The set is Years 8–10 secondary only, so the primary lessons (counting, place value, first steps) receive no Australia item — that is the set's shape, not a reading gap. Australia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (9/9) has been read and placed; the two senior QCE exact-link titles do not block the in-hand class (if exact-link titles are ever ruled in-scope for the courses reckoning, Australia drops to PARTIAL until they are grabbed and read). Into this lesson the placements stock 29 items from Book A1, Book A2, Book A3, Book A4, Book A5, Book A6, Book A7, Book A8, Book A9.

The Class XI file holds only chapters 1–5 of the 26 its own contents lists and the Class XII file only chapters 1–4 of the 19 its contents lists; only the chapters actually present were placed, and both books are cited as the partial excerpts they are.** The 3 teacher's guides are teacher evidence only: their items are named below as teacher evidence and are never counted as pupil teaching. The placements’ named exclusions — front matter, unit openers, objectives lists and the closing national-anthem page in the Grade 5 and Grade 7 books; the Grade 3 book’s cover plate and welcome/mascot pages (PDF pp. 1, 3, 5), its part-opener pages (PDF pp. 55, 85, 107, 111), its closing test “Teste” (printed pp. 125–129 — mixed assessment re-using taught content, no new teaching) and the flag / national-anthem page (PDF p. 130) — and pure exercise / consolidation blocks that teach nothing new are stocked nowhere.
